> DEVELOPER

Implement the following plan:

# Plano: Widget Elementor — BIT Dropdown Button

## Contexto

A página `/publicacoes/` usa botões "DOWNLOAD" com dropdown construídos manualmente via widget HTML do Elementor. O resultado é inconsistente: alguns itens do JetEngine listing têm a estrutura HTML completa (botão estilizado com borda, ícone, dropdown) enquanto outros exibem texto simples "DOWNLOADS" sem nenhum estilo. Converter o componente para um **widget Elementor nativo** resolve a inconsistência e permite controle visual direto no painel do Elementor.

---

## Estado atual

**`bit-dropdown-btn.php`** — apenas enfileira o CSS:
```
add_action('wp_enqueue_scripts', fn() => wp_enqueue_style('bit-dropdown-btn', ...))
```

**`bit-dropdown-btn.css`** — estilos via CSS variables (`--btn-normal-*`).
O ícone usa `mask-image: var(--ucpa-icon-download)` — variável **não definida** em `style.css` (é o motivo do ícone ausente/inconsistente em alguns botões).

**HTML atual (manual no widget HTML):**
```html
<div class="dropdown-btn-wrapper">
  <div class="dropdown-btn-container">
    <button class="dropdown-btn-toggle">
      <div class="dropdown-btn-content">
        <span class="dropdown-btn-label">DOWNLOAD</span>
        <span class="dropdown-btn-icon"></span>
      </div>
    </button>
    <div class="dropdown-btn-menu">
      <a href="...">PT</a>
      <a href="...">EN</a>
    </div>
  </div>
</div>
```

---

## Referência: padrão do projeto

Ver `bit-elementor-svg-widget.php` — o padrão de widget já estabelecido:
- Classe definida dentro do hook `elementor/widgets/register`
- `register_controls()` com tabs Content e Style
- `selectors` nos controles de cor escrevem CSS variables no `{{WRAPPER}}` para reuso dos estilos do CSS file

---

## Implementação

### Arquivo 1 — `bit-dropdown-btn.php` (expandir)

**Adicionar** classe `Bit_Dropdown_Btn_Widget` dentro de `elementor/widgets/register`, mantendo o `wp_enqueue_scripts` existente.

**Controls — Tab Content:**

| Controle | Tipo | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `label` | TEXT | `DOWNLOAD` |
| `links` | REPEATER | — |
| `links > item_label` | TEXT | `Português` |
| `links > item_url` | URL | — |
| `links > item_target` | SELECT | `_self` / `_blank` |

**Controls — Tab Style:**

Todos os controles usam `selectors` apontando para `{{WRAPPER}}` com CSS variables — o CSS file cuida do resto:

| Controle | CSS variable |
|---|---|
| `btn_width` | SLIDER → `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-toggle { width: {{SIZE}}{{UNIT}}; }` |
| `color_bg` | COLOR → `{{WRAPPER}} { --btn-normal-bg: {{VALUE}}; }` |
| `color_txt` | COLOR → `{{WRAPPER}} { --btn-normal-txt: {{VALUE}}; }` |
| `color_bdr` | COLOR → `{{WRAPPER}} { --btn-normal-bdr: {{VALUE}}; }` |
| `color_bg_hv` | COLOR → `{{WRAPPER}} { --btn-normal-bg-hv: {{VALUE}}; }` |
| `color_txt_hv` | COLOR → `{{WRAPPER}} { --btn-normal-txt-hv: {{VALUE}}; }` |
| `color_icn` | COLOR → `{{WRAPPER}} { --btn-normal-icn: {{VALUE}}; }` |

**Render:**
```php
protected function render() {
    $s = $this->get_settings_for_display();
    $label = esc_html($s['label'] ?: 'Download');
    $links = $s['links'] ?? [];
    // Gerar HTML com sanitização
}
```

O ícone SVG (seta para baixo dentro de círculo) será embutido inline no PHP (dados do SVG direto em `render()`), eliminando a dependência de `--ucpa-icon-download`.

### Arquivo 2 — `bit-dropdown-btn.css` (ajuste)

- Remover o comentário que referencia `--ucpa-icon-download` (linha 9)
- Remover as regras `.dropdown-btn-icon` que usam `mask-image: var(--ucpa-icon-download)` — o ícone passa a ser SVG inline renderizado pelo widget
- Manter tudo o mais (layout, hover, dropdown menu)

---

## Arquivos modificados

| Arquivo | Ação |
|---|---|
| `mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.php` | Adicionar widget Elementor (mantém CSS enqueue) |
| `mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.css` | Remover regras `mask-image` do ícone (agora inline) |

Após implementação, copiar ambos para `docker-dev/common/mu-plugins/` (padrão do projeto).

---

## Verificação

1. No Elementor, buscar widget "BIT Download Button" na barra lateral — deve aparecer
2. Inserir widget em qualquer página → verificar preview com label e links configurados
3. Acessar `/publicacoes/` — os botões existentes (HTML widget) continuam funcionando (CSS não mudou)
4. Testar hover: dropdown aparece, cores mudam, ícone renderiza
5. Testar controles de estilo no painel do Elementor: alterar cor de background → reflete no preview

    14→add_action( 'elementor/widgets/register', function ( $widgets_manager ) {
    15→    if ( ! class_exists( '\Elementor\Widget_Base' ) ) return;
    16→
    17→    // Classe definida aqui (dentro do hook) para evitar fatal error:
    18→    // PHP resolve a herança em tempo de execução, não de compilação.
    19→    // Fora do hook, Elementor ainda não carregou → "Class not found".
    20→    class Bureau_SVG_Widget extends \Elementor\Widget_Base {
    21→
    22→    public function get_name()        { return 'bureau_svg'; }
    23→    public function get_title()       { return 'Bureau SVG'; }
    24→    public function get_icon()        { return 'eicon-image'; }

    28→    private function get_svg_options() {
    29→        $svg_dir  = get_stylesheet_directory() . '/svg/';
    30→        $options  = [ '' => '— selecionar —' ];
    31→        $name_map = [
    32→            'logo-concertacao'        => 'Logo Concertação',
    33→            'espiral-concertacao'     => 'Ícone Espiral',
    34→            'espiral-do-conhecimento' => 'Espiral do Conhecimento',
    35→        ];
    36→        foreach ( glob( $svg_dir . '*.svg' ) ?: [] as $file ) {
    37→            $slug             = basename( $file, '.svg' );
    38→            $options[ $slug ] = $name_map[ $slug ] ?? $slug;
    39→        }
    40→        return $options;

   182→
   183→    /**
   184→     * Isola o <style> interno do SVG com um atributo data-scope único,
   185→     * evitando conflito de nomes de classes genéricas (ex: .cls-1) entre
   186→     * múltiplos SVGs inlined na mesma página.
   187→     *
   188→     * Escopo APENAS para SVGs que usam classes genéricas .cls-N.
   189→     * SVGs com classes únicas (ex: .SVGSpiral2026) são retornados sem modificação,
   190→     * preservando seus controles de estilo do Elementor e CSS interno complexo.
   191→     */
   192→    private function scope_svg_styles( string $svg, string $scope_id ): string {
   193→        // Só aplica scoping em SVGs com classes genéricas .cls-N
   194→        if ( ! preg_match( '/\.cls-[\w-]+\s*\{/', $svg ) ) {
   195→            return $svg;
   196→        }
   197→
   198→        // Injetar data-scope no elemento <svg>
   199→        $svg = preg_replace( '/<svg\b/', '<svg data-scope="' . esc_attr( $scope_id ) . '"', $svg, 1 );
   200→
   201→        // Scopar apenas os seletores .cls-* dentro dos blocos <style>
   202→        $svg = preg_replace_callback(
   203→            '/(<style[^>]*>)(.*?)(<\/style>)/is',
   204→            static function ( $m ) use ( $scope_id ) {
   205→                $scoped = preg_replace_callback(
   206→                    '/(\.cls-[\w-]+(?:\s*,\s*\.cls-[\w-]+)*)\s*\{/',
   207→                    static function ( $r ) use ( $scope_id ) {
   208→                        $selectors = array_map( 'trim', explode( ',', $r[1] ) );
   209→                        $prefixed  = array_map(
   210→                            static fn( $s ) => '[data-scope="' . $scope_id . '"] ' . $s,
   211→                            $selectors
   212→                        );
   213→                        return implode( ', ', $prefixed ) . ' {';
   214→                    },
   215→                    $m[2]
   216→                );
   217→                return $m[1] . $scoped . $m[3];
   218→            },
   219→            $svg
   220→        );
   221→
   222→        return $svg;
   223→    }
   224→
   225→    protected function render() {
   226→        $s = $this->get_settings_for_display();
   227→
   228→        $name = ! empty( $s['svg_name'] ) ? $s['svg_name'] : '';
   229→
   230→        if ( empty( $name ) ) return;
   231→
   232→        $file = get_stylesheet_directory() . '/svg/' . $name . '.svg';
   233→
   234→        if ( ! file_exists( $file ) ) return;
   235→
   236→        $svg = file_get_contents( $file );
   237→        if ( empty( $svg ) ) return;
   238→
   239→        // Remover declaração XML (inválida em HTML5 inline)
   240→        $svg = preg_replace( '/<\?xml[^?]*\?>\s*/i', '', $svg );
   241→
   242→        // Isolar CSS interno com data-scope para evitar conflitos entre SVGs inlined
   243→        $svg = $this->scope_svg_styles( $svg, 'bsvg-' . $this->get_id() );
   244→
   245→        // Injetar class no <svg>
   246→        if ( ! empty( $s['svg_class'] ) ) {
   247→            $class = esc_attr( $s['svg_class'] );
   248→            if ( preg_match( '/\bclass="[^"]*"/', $svg ) ) {
   249→                $svg = preg_replace( '/\bclass="([^"]*)"/', 'class="$1 ' . $class . '"', $svg, 1 );
   250→            } else {
   251→                $svg = preg_replace( '/<svg\b/', '<svg class="' . $class . '"', $svg, 1 );
   252→            }
   253→        }
   254→
   255→        // Injetar id no <svg>
   256→        if ( ! empty( $s['svg_id'] ) ) {
   257→            $id = esc_attr( $s['svg_id'] );
   258→            if ( preg_match( '/\bid="[^"]*"/', $svg ) ) {
   259→                $svg = preg_replace( '/\bid="[^"]*"/', 'id="' . $id . '"', $svg, 1 );
   260→            } else {
   261→                $svg = preg_replace( '/<svg\b/', '<svg id="' . $id . '"', $svg, 1 );
   262→            }
   263→        }
   264→
   265→        // Envolver em <a> se link_enabled = 'yes'
   266→        if ( ! empty( $s['link_enabled'] ) && $s['link_enabled'] === 'yes' ) {
   267→            $href   = ! empty( $s['link_url'] )
   268→                ? esc_url( $s['link_url'] )
   269→                : esc_url( apply_filters( 'wpml_home_url', network_home_url( '/' ) ) );
   270→            $target = ( $s['link_target'] ?? '_self' ) === '_blank'
   271→                ? ' target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"'
   272→                : '';
   273→            $svg    = '<a href="' . $href . '"' . $target . '>' . $svg . '</a>';
   274→        }
   275→
   276→        echo $svg; // phpcs:ignore WordPress.Security.EscapeOutput.OutputNotEscaped
   277→    }
   278→} // end class Bureau_SVG_Widget
   279→
   280→    $widgets_manager->register( new Bureau_SVG_Widget() );
   281→} ); // end add_action
   282→

## Summary: JetElements Download Button Plugin Analysis

I've successfully located and analyzed the CrocoBl/CrocoBlock JetElements Download Button plugin in your WordPress installation. Here are the complete findings:

### Exact File Paths

1. **Widget Class**: `/Users/dcambria/scripts/server-tools/v2/docker-dev/sites/concertacao/wordpress/wp-content/plugins/jet-elements/includes/addons/jet-elements-download-button.php`

2. **Download Handler (Encryption/Decryption)**: `/Users/dcambria/scripts/server-tools/v2/docker-dev/sites/concertacao/wordpress/wp-content/plugins/jet-elements/includes/class-jet-elements-download-handler.php`

3. **Render Template**: `/Users/dcambria/scripts/server-tools/v2/docker-dev/sites/concertacao/wordpress/wp-content/plugins/jet-elements/templates/jet-download-button/global/index.php`

4. **CSS Styling**: `/Users/dcambria/scripts/server-tools/v2/docker-dev/sites/concertacao/wordpress/wp-content/plugins/jet-elements/assets/css/addons/jet-download-button.css`

5. **Base Class**: `/Users/dcambria/scripts/server-tools/v2/docker-dev/sites/concertacao/wordpress/wp-content/plugins/jet-elements/includes/base/class-jet-elements-base.php`

---

### Encryption/Obfuscation Mechanism

**Type**: SHA1 hash-based ID encryption with server-side mapping

**Process**:
1. **Encryption** (`encrypt_id()` at line 138-148):
   - Computes: `sha1(NONCE_SALT . $id)`
   - Creates hash-to-ID mapping stored in WP option `jet_elements_download_button_hashes`
   - Returns the SHA1 hash as the encrypted ID

2. **Nonce Key** (`get_nonce_key()` at line 42-57):
   - Uses `NONCE_SALT` constant if defined
   - Falls back to generated key stored in `jet_elements_download_button_encrypt_key_start` option
   - Security key is created once and persists

3. **Decryption** (`decrypt_id()` at line 155-158):
   - Looks up hash in `jet_elements_download_button_hashes` option
   - Returns actual attachment ID or false if hash not found

4. **URL Generation** (`get_download_link()` at line 166-175):
   - Adds query parameter: `?jet_download=[sha1_hash]`
   - Example: `https://example.com/?REDACTED...`

5. **Download Processing** (`process_download()` at line 198-264):
   - Runs on `init` hook at priority 99
   - Checks `$_GET['jet_download']` parameter
   - Decrypts hash to get attachment ID
   - Validates post is attachment type
   - Serves file with proper headers (Content-Type, Content-Disposition, Content-Length)
   - Supports chunked download (1MB chunks) to avoid memory issues

**Security Features**:
- Direct attachment IDs are never exposed in URLs
- Hash-to-ID mapping is server-side only
- Admins can reset hashes via WordPress admin panel (line 67-102)

---

### Widget Class Structure

**Class Name**: `Jet_Elements_Download_Button`

**Inheritance**: Extends `Jet_Elements_Base` (custom Elementor base class)

**Key Methods**:

| Method | Purpose |
|--------|---------|
| `get_name()` | Returns widget slug: `'jet-download-button'` |
| `get_title()` | Returns "Download Button" |
| `get_icon()` | Returns `'jet-elements-icon-download-button'` |
| `get_categories()` | Returns `['cherry']` (Elementor category) |
| `register_controls()` | Registers all Elementor panel controls |
| `_format_label()` | Replaces `%size%` placeholder with file size |
| `render()` | Renders widget by including template |

**Controls** (Settings):

| Control | Type | Description |
|---------|------|-------------|
| `download_file` | TEXT | Media library attachment ID (supports dynamic values) |
| `download_id_encryption` | HIDDEN | Encryption flag (always enabled) |
| `download_label` | TEXT | Main button text (default: "Download", supports %size% placeholder) |
| `download_sub_label` | TEXT | Subtext (default: "%size%") |
| `download_file_name` | SWITCHER | Show/hide filename |
| `download_link_rel` | SWITCHER | Add `rel="nofollow"` attribute |
| `download_icon` | ICON | Icon (supports Font Awesome, custom SVG) |
| `download_icon_position` | SELECT | Icon position: left/right/top/bottom |

---

### Render HTML Structure

**Template File** (index.php, lines 1-51):

```html
<a class="elementor-button elementor-size-md jet-download jet-download-icon-position-[position]" 
   data-e-disable-page-transition="true" 
   href="?jet_download=[sha1_hash]" 
   rel="[nofollow or empty]">
   
   <span class="jet-download__icon jet-download-icon-[position] jet-elements-icon">
      [ICON_HTML]
   </span>
   
   <span class="jet-download__text">
      <span class="jet-download__label">[Formatted Label with %size%]</span>
      <span class="jet-download__file-name">[Filename if enabled]</span>
      <small class="jet-download__sub-label">[Formatted Sub-label]</small>
   </span>
</a>
```

**CSS Classes**:

| Class | Purpose |
|-------|---------|
| `.jet-download` | Main button container (inline-flex) |
| `.jet-download-icon-position-[left\|right\|top\|bottom]` | Icon position modifier |
| `.jet-download__icon` | Icon wrapper |
| `.jet-download__text` | Text container (label + sublabel) |
| `.jet-download__label` | Main download text |
| `.jet-download__sub-label` | Secondary text (usually file size) |
| `.jet-download__file-name` | Filename display |

**Icon Positioning**: CSS flexbox handles reordering via `order` property:
- `left` (default): Icon order 1, Text order 2
- `right`: Icon order 3, Text order 2
- `top`: Icon order 1, Text order 2 (flex-direction: column)
- `bottom`: Icon order 3, Text order 2 (flex-direction: column)

---

### Available Hooks & Filters

**For Template Customization**:
```php
apply_filters( 'jet-elements/download-button/css-scheme', [...] )
```

**For Label Formatting**:
```php
apply_filters( 'jet-elements/download-button/label', $text, $id )
```

**For Control Modification**:
```php
apply_filters( "jet-elements/editor/jet-download-button/include-controls", [] )
apply_filters( "jet-elements/editor/jet-download-button/exclude-controls", [] )
```

**Download Process Hooks**:
- `init` (priority 99): Download handler processes `?jet_download` parameter
- `init` (priority 99): Hash reset handler processes admin reset requests

---

### Key Implementation Details

1. **File Download** (lines 217-263):
   - Clears all output buffers
   - Disables zlib compression
   - Sets proper MIME types (PDF, ZIP, JPG, force-download)
   - Streams file in 1MB chunks to memory-safe
   - Dies after complete download

2. **File Size Display**:
   - Uses `get_file_size()` which calls `get_attached_file()` → `filesize()` → `size_format()`
   - Placeholder `%size%` replaced in both label and sublabel

3. **Dynamic Content Support**:
   - `download_file` control has `'dynamic' => array( 'active' => true )`
   - `download_label` also supports dynamic values
   - Allows conditional rendering based on custom fields/postmeta

4. **Security**:
   - No direct file path exposure in URLs
   - Hash expires/resets when admin clicks reset link
   - Validates file exists before streaming
   - Checks post type is 'attachment'

Sim, é plenamente possível — e é a abordagem ideal. Veja como funciona:

## Como a criptografia do JetElements funciona

O `Jet_Elements_Download_Handler` armazena um mapa `sha1_hash → attachment_id` nas opções do WP. A URL gerada é `/?jet_download=HASH`. O handler processa no hook `init` e serve o arquivo diretamente, sem expor o path nem o ID.

## Abordagem: novo widget no mu-plugin, zero modificação no plugin

O widget `Bit_Dropdown_Btn_Widget` chamaria diretamente:

```php
$handler = Jet_Elements_Download_Handler::get_instance();
$hash    = $handler->encrypt_id( $attachment_id );
$url     = home_url( '/?jet_download=' . $hash );
```

Cada item do repeater teria um campo **Media** (attachment ID) em vez de URL manual. O dropdown renderiza com os links criptografados — o handler do JetElements intercepta automaticamente.

---

## Estrutura do widget revisada

**Tab Content:**

| Controle | Tipo | Detalhe |
|---|---|---|
| `label` | TEXT | Rótulo do botão (`DOWNLOAD`) |
| `links` | REPEATER | — |
| `links > item_label` | TEXT | Ex: `Português`, `English` |
| `links > item_file` | MEDIA | Seleciona da biblioteca de mídia |
| `links > item_fallback_url` | URL | Fallback para arquivos externos |

No render: se `item_file` tiver um ID → usa `encrypt_id()` → `?jet_download=HASH`. Se não tiver → cai no `item_fallback_url` (link direto, sem criptografia).

---

## Requisito

O widget verifica em runtime:

```php
if ( ! class_exists( 'Jet_Elements_Download_Handler' ) ) {
    // JetElements não está ativo — renderiza fallback simples
}
```

### 4. JetElements Download Button Widget Icon Approach

**Icon Method** (from `class-jet-elements-base.php`, lines 588-680):
- Uses `_icon()` method which dispatches to context-specific render (`_render_icon()`)

### Key Findings

1. **No hard-coded download icon in JetElements** - The widget accepts any icon via Elementor's icon picker
2. **Theme applies download arrow mask** - The `.dropdown-btn-container .dropdown-btn-icon` uses CSS mask with the circle+arrow SVG
3. **Icon rendering is flexible** - JetElements supports both modern Elementor icons (SVG) and Font Awesome font icons
4. **SVG is data URI** - No separate file needed; the icon is embedded as URL-encoded SVG in the CSS variable

     1→# Visual Companion Guide
     2→
     3→Browser-based visual brainstorming companion for showing mockups, diagrams, and options.
     4→
     5→## When to Use
     6→
     7→Decide per-question, not per-session. The test: **would the user understand this better by seeing it than reading it?**
     8→
     9→**Use the browser** when the content itself is visual:
    10→
    11→- **UI mockups** — wireframes, layouts, navigation structures, component designs
    12→- **Architecture diagrams** — system components, data flow, relationship maps
    13→- **Side-by-side visual comparisons** — comparing two layouts, two color schemes, two design directions
    14→- **Design polish** — when the question is about look and feel, spacing, visual hierarchy
    15→- **Spatial relationships** — state machines, flowcharts, entity relationships rendered as diagrams
    16→
    17→**Use the terminal** when the content is text or tabular:
    18→
    19→- **Requirements and scope questions** — "what does X mean?", "which features are in scope?"
    20→- **Conceptual A/B/C choices** — picking between approaches described in words
    21→- **Tradeoff lists** — pros/cons, comparison tables
    22→- **Technical decisions** — API design, data modeling, architectural approach selection
    23→- **Clarifying questions** — anything where the answer is words, not a visual preference
    24→
    25→A question *about* a UI topic is not automatically a visual question. "What kind of wizard do you want?" is conceptual — use the terminal. "Which of these wizard layouts feels right?" is visual — use the browser.
    26→
    27→## How It Works
    28→
    29→The server watches a directory for HTML files and serves the newest one to the browser. You write HTML content, the user sees it in their browser and can click to select options. Selections are recorded to a `.events` file that you read on your next turn.
    30→
    31→**Content fragments vs full documents:** If your HTML file starts with `<!DOCTYPE` or `<html`, the server serves it as-is (just injects the helper script). Otherwise, the server automatically wraps your content in the frame template — adding the header, CSS theme, selection indicator, and all interactive infrastructure. **Write content fragments by default.** Only write full documents when you need complete control over the page.
    32→
    33→## Starting a Session
    34→
    35→```bash
    36→# Start server with persistence (mockups saved to project)
    37→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    38→
    39→# Returns: {"type":"server-started","port":52341,"url":"http://localhost:52341",
    40→#           "screen_dir":"/path/to/project/.superpowers/brainstorm/12345-1706000000"}
    41→```
    42→
    43→Save `screen_dir` from the response. Tell user to open the URL.
    44→
    45→**Finding connection info:** The server writes its startup JSON to `$SCREEN_DIR/.server-info`. If you launched the server in the background and didn't capture stdout, read that file to get the URL and port. When using `--project-dir`, check `<project>/.superpowers/brainstorm/` for the session directory.
    46→
    47→**Note:** Pass the project root as `--project-dir` so mockups persist in `.superpowers/brainstorm/` and survive server restarts. Without it, files go to `/tmp` and get cleaned up. Remind the user to add `.superpowers/` to `.gitignore` if it's not already there.
    48→
    49→**Launching the server by platform:**
    50→
    51→**Claude Code:**
    52→```bash
    53→# Default mode works — the script backgrounds the server itself
    54→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    55→```
    56→
    57→**Codex:**
    58→```bash
    59→# Codex reaps background processes. The script auto-detects CODEX_CI and
    60→# switches to foreground mode. Run it normally — no extra flags needed.
    61→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    62→```
    63→
    64→**Gemini CLI:**
    65→```bash
    66→# Use --foreground and set is_background: true on your shell tool call
    67→# so the process survives across turns
    68→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project --foreground
    69→```
    70→
    71→**Other environments:** The server must keep running in the background across conversation turns. If your environment reaps detached processes, use `--foreground` and launch the command with your platform's background execution mechanism.
    72→
    73→If the URL is unreachable from your browser (common in remote/containerized setups), bind a non-loopback host:
    74→
    75→```bash
    76→scripts/start-server.sh \
    77→  --project-dir /path/to/project \
    78→  --host 0.0.0.0 \
    79→  --url-host localhost
    80→```
    81→
    82→Use `--url-host` to control what hostname is printed in the returned URL JSON.
    83→
    84→## The Loop
    85→
    86→1. **Write HTML** to a new file in `screen_dir`:
    87→   - Use semantic filenames: `platform.html`, `visual-style.html`, `layout.html`
    88→   - **Never reuse filenames** — each screen gets a fresh file
    89→   - Use Write tool — **never use cat/heredoc** (dumps noise into terminal)
    90→   - Server automatically serves the newest file
    91→
    92→2. **Tell user what to expect and end your turn:**
    93→   - Remind them of the URL (every step, not just first)
    94→   - Give a brief text summary of what's on screen (e.g., "Showing 3 layout options for the homepage")
    95→   - Ask them to respond in the terminal: "Take a look and let me know what you think. Click to select an option if you'd like."
    96→
    97→3. **On your next turn** — after the user responds in the terminal:
    98→   - Read `$SCREEN_DIR/.events` if it exists — this contains the user's browser interactions (clicks, selections) as JSON lines
    99→   - Merge with the user's terminal text to get the full picture
   100→   - The terminal message is the primary feedback; `.events` provides structured interaction data
   101→
   102→4. **Iterate or advance** — if feedback changes current screen, write a new file (e.g., `layout-v2.html`). Only move to the next question when the current step is validated.
   103→
   104→5. **Unload when returning to terminal** — when the next step doesn't need the browser (e.g., a clarifying question, a tradeoff discussion), push a waiting screen to clear the stale content:
   105→
   106→   ```html
   107→   <!-- filename: waiting.html (or waiting-2.html, etc.) -->
   108→   <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;min-height:60vh">
   109→     <p class="subtitle">Continuing in terminal...</p>
   110→   </div>
   111→   ```
   112→
   113→   This prevents the user from staring at a resolved choice while the conversation has moved on. When the next visual question comes up, push a new content file as usual.
   114→
   115→6. Repeat until done.
   116→
   117→## Writing Content Fragments
   118→
   119→Write just the content that goes inside the page. The server wraps it in the frame template automatically (header, theme CSS, selection indicator, and all interactive infrastructure).
   120→
   121→**Minimal example:**
   122→
   123→```html
   124→<h2>Which layout works better?</h2>
   125→<p class="subtitle">Consider readability and visual hierarchy</p>
   126→
   127→<div class="options">
   128→  <div class="option" data-choice="a" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   129→    <div class="letter">A</div>
   130→    <div class="content">
   131→      <h3>Single Column</h3>
   132→      <p>Clean, focused reading experience</p>
   133→    </div>
   134→  </div>
   135→  <div class="option" data-choice="b" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   136→    <div class="letter">B</div>
   137→    <div class="content">
   138→      <h3>Two Column</h3>
   139→      <p>Sidebar navigation with main content</p>
   140→    </div>
   141→  </div>
   142→</div>
   143→```
   144→
   145→That's it. No `<html>`, no CSS, no `<script>` tags needed. The server provides all of that.
   146→
   147→## CSS Classes Available
   148→
   149→The frame template provides these CSS classes for your content:
   150→
   151→### Options (A/B/C choices)
   152→
   153→```html
   154→<div class="options">
   155→  <div class="option" data-choice="a" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   156→    <div class="letter">A</div>
   157→    <div class="content">
   158→      <h3>Title</h3>
   159→      <p>Description</p>
   160→    </div>
   161→  </div>
   162→</div>
   163→```
   164→
   165→**Multi-select:** Add `data-multiselect` to the container to let users select multiple options. Each click toggles the item. The indicator bar shows the count.
   166→
   167→```html
   168→<div class="options" data-multiselect>
   169→  <!-- same option markup — users can select/deselect multiple -->
   170→</div>
   171→```
   172→
   173→### Cards (visual designs)
   174→
   175→```html
   176→<div class="cards">
   177→  <div class="card" data-choice="design1" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   178→    <div class="card-image"><!-- mockup content --></div>
   179→    <div class="card-body">
   180→      <h3>Name</h3>
   181→      <p>Description</p>
   182→    </div>
   183→  </div>
   184→</div>
   185→```
   186→
   187→### Mockup container
   188→
   189→```html
   190→<div class="mockup">
   191→  <div class="mockup-header">Preview: Dashboard Layout</div>
   192→  <div class="mockup-body"><!-- your mockup HTML --></div>
   193→</div>
   194→```
   195→
   196→### Split view (side-by-side)
   197→
   198→```html
   199→<div class="split">
   200→  <div class="mockup"><!-- left --></div>
   201→  <div class="mockup"><!-- right --></div>
   202→</div>
   203→```
   204→
   205→### Pros/Cons
   206→
   207→```html
   208→<div class="pros-cons">
   209→  <div class="pros"><h4>Pros</h4><ul><li>Benefit</li></ul></div>
   210→  <div class="cons"><h4>Cons</h4><ul><li>Drawback</li></ul></div>
   211→</div>
   212→```
   213→
   214→### Mock elements (wireframe building blocks)
   215→
   216→```html
   217→<div class="mock-nav">Logo | Home | About | Contact</div>
   218→<div style="display: flex;">
   219→  <div class="mock-sidebar">Navigation</div>
   220→  <div class="mock-content">Main content area</div>
   221→</div>
   222→<button class="mock-button">Action Button</button>
   223→<input class="mock-input" placeholder="Input field">
   224→<div class="placeholder">Placeholder area</div>
   225→```
   226→
   227→### Typography and sections
   228→
   229→- `h2` — page title
   230→- `h3` — section heading
   231→- `.subtitle` — secondary text below title
   232→- `.section` — content block with bottom margin
   233→- `.label` — small uppercase label text
   234→
   235→## Browser Events Format
   236→
   237→When the user clicks options in the browser, their interactions are recorded to `$SCREEN_DIR/.events` (one JSON object per line). The file is cleared automatically when you push a new screen.
   238→
   239→```jsonl
   240→{"type":"click","choice":"a","text":"Option A - Simple Layout","timestamp":1706000101}
   241→{"type":"click","choice":"c","text":"Option C - Complex Grid","timestamp":1706000108}
   242→{"type":"click","choice":"b","text":"Option B - Hybrid","timestamp":1706000115}
   243→```
   244→
   245→The full event stream shows the user's exploration path — they may click multiple options before settling. The last `choice` event is typically the final selection, but the pattern of clicks can reveal hesitation or preferences worth asking about.
   246→
   247→If `.events` doesn't exist, the user didn't interact with the browser — use only their terminal text.
   248→
   249→## Design Tips
   250→
   251→- **Scale fidelity to the question** — wireframes for layout, polish for polish questions
   252→- **Explain the question on each page** — "Which layout feels more professional?" not just "Pick one"
   253→- **Iterate before advancing** — if feedback changes current screen, write a new version
   254→- **2-4 options max** per screen
   255→- **Use real content when it matters** — for a photography portfolio, use actual images (Unsplash). Placeholder content obscures design issues.
   256→- **Keep mockups simple** — focus on layout and structure, not pixel-perfect design
   257→
   258→## File Naming
   259→
   260→- Use semantic names: `platform.html`, `visual-style.html`, `layout.html`
   261→- Never reuse filenames — each screen must be a new file
   262→- For iterations: append version suffix like `layout-v2.html`, `layout-v3.html`
   263→- Server serves newest file by modification time
   264→
   265→## Cleaning Up
   266→
   267→```bash
   268→scripts/stop-server.sh $SCREEN_DIR
   269→```
   270→
   271→If the session used `--project-dir`, mockup files persist in `.superpowers/brainstorm/` for later reference. Only `/tmp` sessions get deleted on stop.
   272→
   273→## Reference
   274→
   275→- Frame template (CSS reference): `scripts/frame-template.html`
   276→- Helper script (client-side): `scripts/helper.js`
   277→

Whenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

---

## Design — BIT Dropdown Button v3.0.0

### Arquivo
`mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.php` (versão atual v2.0.0 → v3.0.0)

### Criptografia
O widget chama `Jet_Elements_Download_Handler::get_instance()->encrypt_id($attachment_id)` para gerar `?jet_download=HASH`. O handler do JetElements já intercepta e serve o arquivo. Se o JetElements não estiver ativo, cai para o `item_fallback_url` sem criptografia.

### Tab Content

| Controle | Tipo | Detalhe |
|---|---|---|
| `label` | TEXT | Rótulo do botão, default `DOWNLOAD` |
| `links` (repeater) | REPEATER | — |
| `↳ item_label` | TEXT | Ex: `Português`, `English` |
| `↳ item_file` | MEDIA | Seleciona attachment da biblioteca |
| `↳ item_fallback_url` | URL | Usado quando não há attachment (externo) |

### Tab Style

**Botão — Dimensões**
- `btn_width` SLIDER (px/%)
- `btn_border_radius` DIMENSIONS
- `btn_padding` DIMENSIONS
- `btn_box_shadow` BOX_SHADOW

**Cores — Normal**
- `color_bg`, `color_txt`, `color_bdr` → CSS vars `--btn-normal-bg/txt/bdr`

**Cores — Hover**
- `color_bg_hv`, `color_txt_hv`, `color_bdr_hv` → CSS vars `--btn-normal-*-hv`

**Ícone**
- `icon` ICONS (picker nativo Elementor — default = SVG do círculo com seta)
- `icon_size` SLIDER (px)
- `color_icn`, `color_icn_hv` → CSS vars `--btn-normal-icn / --btn-normal-icon-hv`

**Tipografia**
- `label_typography` TYPOGRAPHY GROUP (label do botão)
- `menu_typography` TYPOGRAPHY GROUP (itens do dropdown)

**Menu Dropdown**
- `menu_min_width` SLIDER (px/%)
- `menu_item_padding` DIMENSIONS
- `menu_color_bg`, `menu_color_txt` → fundo e texto normais
- `menu_color_txt_hv`, `menu_color_bg_hv` → hover
- `menu_color_bdr`, `menu_color_divider` → borda e separador entre itens

### CSS
O `bit-dropdown-btn.css` recebe apenas ajustes para suportar os novos seletores (`border-radius`, `padding`, `box-shadow`, `min-width` do menu). O ícone continua inline via `render()`, agora via `Icons_Manager::render_icon()`.

### Render
```
div.dropdown-btn-wrapper
  div.dropdown-btn-container
    button.dropdown-btn-toggle
      div.dropdown-btn-content
        span.dropdown-btn-label   ← label
        span.dropdown-btn-icon    ← ícone via Icons_Manager
    div.dropdown-btn-menu
      a[href="?jet_download=HASH"] ← criptografado
      a[href="?jet_download=HASH"] ← criptografado
```

Now I have a complete picture. Let me compile the review.

---

## ISSUES FOUND

The spec is well-structured and the encryption mechanism description is accurate. However, there are several issues ranging from critical to suggestions that must be addressed before implementation.

---

### CRITICAL

**1. Wrong method called for URL generation**

The spec (Section "Mecanismo de Criptografia" and "Lógica de URL por item") instructs calling `encrypt_id($id)` directly, but that method is declared `private` in `Jet_Elements_Download_Handler`. The correct public API is `get_download_link( $id )`, which internally calls `encrypt_id()` and returns the full URL via `add_query_arg`. The implementation code must call:

```php
jet_elements_download_handler()->get_download_link( $attachment_id )
```

Not a hand-rolled `home_url('/?jet_download=' . encrypt_id($id))`. If the spec is treated literally, implementation will fail with a fatal error or require reflection hacks.

The spec must either: (a) update the "Lógica de URL" table to reference `get_download_link()`, or (b) explicitly note that `get_download_link()` is the entry point and the internal `encrypt_id` call is just illustrative of the mechanism.

**2. JetElements availability check is incomplete**

The spec states: "se `Jet_Elements_Download_Handler` não estiver disponível… usa `item_fallback_url`". But the handler is a singleton that must also have been initialized (its `init()` method called) to register the `process_download` hook. The class existing does not guarantee the handler is active. The correct check is:

```php
function_exists( 'jet_elements_download_handler' ) && jet_elements_download_handler() instanceof Jet_Elements_Download_Handler
```

The spec should specify which condition gates the fallback, or at minimum note that the function `jet_elements_download_handler()` must exist.

**3. MEDIA control does not return a simple ID — attachment sub-key required**

The spec Table (Tab Content) shows `item_file` as type MEDIA with no note about how to read the attachment ID. Elementor's `MEDIA` control returns an array: `[ 'id' => int, 'url' => string ]`. The render logic must access `$link['item_file']['id']` (not `$link['item_file']`). Without this note, an implementer could write a direct cast to `(int)$link['item_file']` which always yields 0 and always triggers the fallback. The spec must document this explicitly in the "Lógica de URL por item" section.

---

### IMPORTANT

**4. Icon control default value: vague and likely unimplementable**

Section "Seção: Ícone" states: `Default do ícone: SVG inline do círculo com seta... pré-configurado como default no controle ICONS via skin: 'caret' ou SVG embutido como valor padrão.`

The `ICONS` control in Elementor does not accept an SVG string as its default value. The `default` key accepts a value structure like `['value' => 'eicon-download', 'library' => 'eicons']`. The v2 implementation avoids this entirely by using a private `get_download_icon_svg()` method that renders a hardcoded SVG when the ICONS control has no selection.

The spec must clarify: should the widget keep the same pattern as v2 (private hardcoded SVG as fallback when the ICONS control is empty), or should it use a specific Elementor icon library icon as default? The phrase "SVG embutido como valor padrão" is ambiguous and will lead to either an incorrect implementation or wasted time.

**5. CSS spec says to remove `width: 220px` and `border-radius: 4px` but v2 uses them at specificity (0,2,1)**

The existing CSS rule `.dropdown-btn-container button.dropdown-btn-toggle` uses specificity (0,2,1), which is documented inline in the CSS file itself. The Elementor `selectors` output generates rules on `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-toggle` which is approximately (0,1,1). If the hardcoded values are removed but the Elementor control is left empty/zero (no value set), the button loses its dimensions entirely. The spec must state what default values the Elementor controls should carry (`btn_width` default: 220px is already in v2, but `btn_border_radius` and `btn_padding` have no defaults specified). Add default values for `btn_border_radius` (4px) and `btn_padding` (15px 25px) to the Tab Style control table.

**6. `color_bdr_hv` CSS variable name inconsistency**

In the "Seção: Cores — Hover" table, `color_bdr_hv` maps to `--btn-normal-border-hv`. But in "Seção: Cores — Normal", `color_bdr` maps to `--btn-normal-bdr`. The naming pattern is inconsistent (`-bdr` vs `-border-`). The existing CSS (line 8 of `bit-dropdown-btn.css`) and v2 code already use `--btn-normal-border-hv`. This inconsistency between the two hover color var names should be acknowledged explicitly in the spec so the implementer does not "fix" the existing variable name and break the CSS.

**7. Repeater default items: field name mismatch**

The v2 default for the repeater's URL field is `'item_url'`. The v3 spec renames this field to `'item_file'` (MEDIA) + `'item_fallback_url'` (URL). The spec must include the updated `default` array for the `links` repeater control with the new field keys, since copy-pasting from v2 will silently use the wrong keys and render no URLs.

---

### SUGGESTIONS

**8. Verification step 9 is not testable as written**

"JetElements inativo: widget renderiza com fallback URL sem erro fatal" — in a mu-plugins context, the check `class_exists('Jet_Elements_Download_Handler')` happens at render time. The spec should clarify how to simulate this state locally (deactivate JetElements plugin) and what the expected output is (renders `item_fallback_url` as `<a href>`, or skips item entirely if both are empty).

**9. No `nofollow` attribute specified for encrypted download links**

The render HTML example shows `rel="noopener"` only. For download URLs the spec should clarify whether `rel="nofollow"` should also be added, since the purpose of the encryption is explicitly to prevent search engine crawling. The fallback URL path already had `nofollow` support in v2.

**10. No mention of the `common/mu-plugins/` mirror step in the implementation workflow**

Per `/Users/dcambria/scripts/server-tools/v2/docker-dev/sites/CLAUDE.md`, every mu-plugin change must be copied to `docker-dev/common/mu-plugins/` and committed in the server-tools repo. The spec's "Arquivos Modificados" table lists this, which is correct — but there is no corresponding verification step in Section "Verificação" confirming the mirror was done. Add a step 10 to the verification checklist.

---

### Summary

| Severity | Count | Items |
|---|---|---|
| Critical | 3 | #1 wrong method, #2 incomplete availability check, #3 MEDIA control sub-key |
| Important | 4 | #4 icon default ambiguity, #5 missing control defaults, #6 CSS var naming inconsistency, #7 repeater default field keys |
| Suggestions | 3 | #8 untestable step, #9 nofollow for encrypted links, #10 missing mirror verification step |

The spec requires corrections to items #1 through #7 before implementation can proceed safely. Items #1 and #3 in particular will cause silent runtime failures — the download URL will never be generated — unless the spec is clarified.

id: toolu_015kVG9XTkwm6yaZgyJA1hAU
```
The spec has been fully read. Now I will verify each of the 7 previously reported issues against the current document.

**Issue 1 — Wrong method (`encrypt_id` private → `get_download_link` public)**

Line 36: `jet_elements_download_handler()->get_download_link( $attachment_id )` is correctly specified, with an explicit inline comment on line 35 saying "não chamar `encrypt_id()` diretamente (método private)". Fixed.

**Issue 2 — Incomplete JetElements availability check**

Lines 46–51: The guard now uses `function_exists( 'jet_elements_download_handler' ) && jet_elements_download_handler() instanceof Jet_Elements_Download_Handler`, with a prose comment explaining why `class_exists()` alone is insufficient. The same double-guard is reproduced in the render logic on lines 91–93. Fixed.

**Issue 3 — MEDIA control sub-key (`item_file['id']`)**

Line 84 explicitly states "Acessar sempre via `$link['item_file']['id']`". Line 87 shows `(int) ( $link['item_file']['id'] ?? 0 )`. Table row at line 71 documents the MEDIA default as `['id' => 0, 'url' => '']`. Fixed.

**Issue 4 — Icon default ambiguity**

Line 132: `icon` control default is `['value' => 'eicon-download', 'library' => 'eicons']` — both keys present, unambiguous. Fixed.

**Issue 5 — Missing control defaults (`border_radius`, `padding`)**

Line 108: `btn_border_radius` DIMENSIONS default documented as `4px (todos lados)`.
Line 109: `btn_padding` DIMENSIONS default documented as `15px top/bottom, 25px left/right`. Fixed.

**Issue 6 — CSS var naming inconsistency (`--btn-normal-border-hv`)**

Line 126: The name `--btn-normal-border-hv` is retained with an explicit parenthetical annotation: "(manter nome existente — inconsistência herdada do CSS)". The spec now acknowledges the inconsistency and gives a clear implementation instruction. Fixed.

**Issue 7 — Repeater default field keys**

Lines 76–79: The repeater `default` array uses the exact control IDs `item_label`, `item_file`, `item_fallback_url` with correctly structured sub-arrays including both `['id' => 0, 'url' => '']` for the MEDIA control and `['url' => '']` for the URL control. Fixed.

**Additional pass — checking for new issues**

One remaining concern worth flagging as a suggestion: the `item_fallback_url` URL control default in the repeater is `['url' => '']`, but the render logic on line 95 applies `esc_url( $fallback_url )` where `$fallback_url` is already a raw URL string (extracted from the array on line 88). This is correct. No inconsistency here.

The `get_default_icon_svg()` SVG (lines 141–147) declares `fill="currentColor"` at the root element but the path data describes a shape with an interior cutout. `fill="currentColor"` on the `<svg>` element combined with a single `<path>` with an implicit `fill-rule: nonzero` will fill the cutout. If the intent is a donut/hollow arrow shape, the spec should specify `fill-rule="evenodd"` on the path. This is a new minor issue not present in the previous review.

The `is_external` check on line 198 is referenced for `item_fallback_url`, but the control table (line 72) defines it only as type URL with no mention of an `is_external` toggle. Elementor's URL control does include that toggle by default, but since no explicit control configuration disabling it is shown, this is consistent. Not an issue.

---

**ISSUES FOUND**

All 7 previously reported issues have been resolved. One new minor issue was identified:

**Suggestion — SVG fill-rule for hollow icon (line 145–146)**

The default icon SVG uses `fill="currentColor"` at the SVG level with a single path that appears to describe a filled disc with a triangle punched out (`fill-rule: nonzero` applies by default). If the visual intent is a hollow interior (the downward arrow is the negative space within a filled circle), the path requires `fill-rule="evenodd"` on the `<path>` element; otherwise the "hole" fills solid. This is low-risk since the SVG is a private method and can be verified visually immediately, but the spec should document the intended fill-rule to avoid ambiguity during implementation.

Corrected line:
```php
// In get_default_icon_svg(), change:
'<path d="M11,0C4.92,0...">'
// to:
'<path fill-rule="evenodd" d="M11,0C4.92,0...">'
```

**Severity: Suggestion** — does not block implementation, but should be validated at the visual verification step (step 5 of the verification checklist at line 225).

All critical and important issues from the prior review are closed. The spec is implementation-ready pending this one clarification.

     1→# Plan Document Reviewer Prompt Template
     2→
     3→Use this template when dispatching a plan document reviewer subagent.
     4→
     5→**Purpose:** Verify the plan chunk is complete, matches the spec, and has proper task decomposition.
     6→
     7→**Dispatch after:** Each plan chunk is written
     8→
     9→```
    10→Task tool (general-purpose):
    11→  description: "Review plan chunk N"
    12→  prompt: |
    13→    You are a plan document reviewer. Verify this plan chunk is complete and ready for implementation.
    14→
    15→    **Plan chunk to review:** [PLAN_FILE_PATH] - Chunk N only
    16→    **Spec for reference:** [SPEC_FILE_PATH]
    17→
    18→    ## What to Check
    19→
    20→    | Category | What to Look For |
    21→    |----------|------------------|
    22→    | Completeness | TODOs, placeholders, incomplete tasks, missing steps |
    23→    | Spec Alignment | Chunk covers relevant spec requirements, no scope creep |
    24→    | Task Decomposition | Tasks atomic, clear boundaries, steps actionable |
    25→    | File Structure | Files have clear single responsibilities, split by responsibility not layer |
    26→    | File Size | Would any new or modified file likely grow large enough to be hard to reason about as a whole? |
    27→    | Task Syntax | Checkbox syntax (`- [ ]`) on steps for tracking |
    28→    | Chunk Size | Each chunk under 1000 lines |
    29→
    30→    ## CRITICAL
    31→
    32→    Look especially hard for:
    33→    - Any TODO markers or placeholder text
    34→    - Steps that say "similar to X" without actual content
    35→    - Incomplete task definitions
    36→    - Missing verification steps or expected outputs
    37→    - Files planned to hold multiple responsibilities or likely to grow unwieldy
    38→
    39→    ## Output Format
    40→
    41→    ## Plan Review - Chunk N
    42→
    43→    **Status:** Approved | Issues Found
    44→
    45→    **Issues (if any):**
    46→    - [Task X, Step Y]: [specific issue] - [why it matters]
    47→
    48→    **Recommendations (advisory):**
    49→    - [suggestions that don't block approval]
    50→```
    51→
    52→**Reviewer returns:** Status, Issues (if any), Recommendations
    53→

     1→# Design: BIT Dropdown Button v3.0.0
     2→
     3→**Data:** 2026-03-12
     4→**Autor:** Bureau IT
     5→**Status:** Aprovado
     6→
     7→---
     8→
     9→## Contexto
    10→
    11→A página `/publicacoes/` usa botões "DOWNLOAD" com dropdown para múltiplos arquivos (PT/EN). O widget atual (v2.0.0) usa links diretos, expondo URLs de arquivos para rastreamento por buscadores.
    12→
    13→Esta versão integra a criptografia nativa do JetElements (plugin CrocoBl/CrocoBlock) para gerar URLs ofuscadas no formato `?jet_download=SHA1_HASH`, impedindo que buscadores rastreiem os links diretos de download.
    14→
    15→---
    16→
    17→## Arquivos Modificados
    18→
    19→| Arquivo | Ação |
    20→|---|---|
    21→| `wordpress/wp-content/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.php` | Atualizar de v2.0.0 → v3.0.0 |
    22→| `wordpress/wp-content/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.css` | Ajustes para novos seletores |
    23→| `docker-dev/common/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.php` | Espelho (cópia após alteração) |
    24→| `docker-dev/common/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.css` | Espelho (cópia após alteração) |
    25→
    26→---
    27→
    28→## Mecanismo de Criptografia
    29→
    30→O `Jet_Elements_Download_Handler` (JetElements) armazena um mapa `SHA1_HASH → attachment_id` na opção WP `jet_elements_download_button_hashes`. A chave usa `NONCE_SALT`.
    31→
    32→**API pública correta (único ponto de entrada):**
    33→
    34→```php
    35→// Gera a URL completa com hash — não chamar encrypt_id() diretamente (método private)
    36→jet_elements_download_handler()->get_download_link( $attachment_id )
    37→// Retorna: "https://site.com/?jet_download=abc123sha1hash"
    38→```
    39→
    40→O handler intercepta no hook `init` (priority 99), decripta o hash, valida o attachment e serve o arquivo via headers HTTP com streaming em chunks de 1MB.
    41→
    42→**Verificação de disponibilidade:**
    43→
    44→```php
    45→// Condição correta — class_exists() sozinho não garante que o handler foi inicializado
    46→if ( function_exists( 'jet_elements_download_handler' )
    47→     && jet_elements_download_handler() instanceof Jet_Elements_Download_Handler ) {
    48→    $url = jet_elements_download_handler()->get_download_link( $attachment_id );
    49→} else {
    50→    $url = $item_fallback_url; // link direto sem criptografia
    51→}
    52→```
    53→
    54→---
    55→
    56→## Widget: `Bit_Dropdown_Btn_Widget`
    57→
    58→**Classe:** `Bit_Dropdown_Btn_Widget`
    59→**Slug:** `bit_dropdown_btn`
    60→**Título:** `BIT Download Button`
    61→**Ícone:** `eicon-button`
    62→**Categoria:** `general`
    63→
    64→### Tab Content
    65→
    66→| Controle | ID | Tipo | Default |
    67→|---|---|---|---|
    68→| Rótulo do botão | `label` | TEXT | `DOWNLOAD` |
    69→| Links (repeater) | `links` | REPEATER | 2 itens padrão (ver abaixo) |
    70→| ↳ Rótulo do item | `item_label` | TEXT | `Português` / `English` |
    71→| ↳ Arquivo (media) | `item_file` | MEDIA | `['id' => 0, 'url' => '']` |
    72→| ↳ URL fallback | `item_fallback_url` | URL | `['url' => '']` |
    73→
    74→**Default do repeater (campo `default` no controle `links`):**
    75→```php
    76→'default' => [
    77→    [ 'item_label' => 'Português', 'item_file' => [ 'id' => 0, 'url' => '' ], 'item_fallback_url' => [ 'url' => '' ] ],
    78→    [ 'item_label' => 'English',   'item_file' => [ 'id' => 0, 'url' => '' ], 'item_fallback_url' => [ 'url' => '' ] ],
    79→],
    80→```
    81→
    82→**Lógica de URL por item no `render()`:**
    83→
    84→O controle MEDIA retorna array `['id' => int, 'url' => string]`. Acessar sempre via `$link['item_file']['id']`.
    85→
    86→```php
    87→$attachment_id = (int) ( $link['item_file']['id'] ?? 0 );
    88→$fallback_url  = $link['item_fallback_url']['url'] ?? '';
    89→
    90→if ( $attachment_id > 0
    91→     && function_exists( 'jet_elements_download_handler' )
    92→     && jet_elements_download_handler() instanceof Jet_Elements_Download_Handler ) {
    93→    $href = jet_elements_download_handler()->get_download_link( $attachment_id );
    94→} elseif ( ! empty( $fallback_url ) ) {
    95→    $href = esc_url( $fallback_url );
    96→} else {
    97→    continue; // item sem URL válida — não renderiza
    98→}
    99→```
   100→
   101→### Tab Style
   102→
   103→#### Seção: Botão — Dimensões
   104→
   105→| Controle | ID | Tipo | Default | Seletor CSS |
   106→|---|---|---|---|---|
   107→| Largura | `btn_width` | SLIDER (px/%) | `220px` | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-toggle { width: {{SIZE}}{{UNIT}}; }` |
   108→| Border radius | `btn_border_radius` | DIMENSIONS | `4px` (todos lados) | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-toggle { border-radius: {{TOP}}{{UNIT}} {{RIGHT}}{{UNIT}} {{BOTTOM}}{{UNIT}} {{LEFT}}{{UNIT}}; }` |
   109→| Padding | `btn_padding` | DIMENSIONS | `15px` top/bottom, `25px` left/right | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-toggle { padding: {{TOP}}{{UNIT}} {{RIGHT}}{{UNIT}} {{BOTTOM}}{{UNIT}} {{LEFT}}{{UNIT}}; }` |
   110→| Box shadow | `btn_box_shadow` | BOX_SHADOW | — | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-toggle` |
   111→
   112→#### Seção: Cores — Normal
   113→
   114→| Controle | ID | CSS var |
   115→|---|---|---|
   116→| Fundo | `color_bg` | `--btn-normal-bg` |
   117→| Texto | `color_txt` | `--btn-normal-txt` |
   118→| Borda | `color_bdr` | `--btn-normal-bdr` |
   119→
   120→#### Seção: Cores — Hover
   121→
   122→| Controle | ID | CSS var |
   123→|---|---|---|
   124→| Fundo hover | `color_bg_hv` | `--btn-normal-bg-hv` |
   125→| Texto hover | `color_txt_hv` | `--btn-normal-txt-hv` |
   126→| Borda hover | `color_bdr_hv` | `--btn-normal-border-hv` (manter nome existente — inconsistência herdada do CSS) |
   127→
   128→#### Seção: Ícone
   129→
   130→| Controle | ID | Tipo | Default | Seletor/Var |
   131→|---|---|---|---|---|
   132→| Ícone | `icon` | ICONS | `['value' => 'eicon-download', 'library' => 'eicons']` | renderizado via `Icons_Manager::render_icon()` |
   133→| Tamanho | `icon_size` | SLIDER (px) | `30px` | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-icon svg, {{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-icon i { width: {{SIZE}}{{UNIT}}; height: {{SIZE}}{{UNIT}}; }` |
   134→| Cor normal | `color_icn` | COLOR | — | `{{WRAPPER}} { --btn-normal-icn: {{VALUE}}; }` |
   135→| Cor hover | `color_icn_hv` | COLOR | — | `{{WRAPPER}} { --btn-normal-icon-hv: {{VALUE}}; }` |
   136→
   137→**Fallback de ícone:** quando o controle `icon` estiver vazio ou não retornar HTML, renderizar o SVG hardcoded do círculo com seta (mesmo padrão da v2, via método privado `get_default_icon_svg()`). Isso garante que o botão sempre exibe um ícone sem depender da seleção do usuário.
   138→
   139→O SVG padrão (do `--ucpa-icon-download`):
   140→```php
   141→private function get_default_icon_svg(): string {
   142→    return '<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" viewBox="0 0 22 22" '
   143→         . 'aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" fill="currentColor">'
   144→         . '<path fill-rule="evenodd" d="M11,0C4.92,0,0,4.92,0,11s4.92,11,11,11,11-4.92,11-11'
   145→         . 'S17.08,0,11,0Zm.13,8.42h3.5l-1.75,3.03-1.75,3.03-1.75-3.03-1.75-3.03h3.5Z"/>'
   146→         . '</svg>';
   147→}
   148→```
   149→
   150→#### Seção: Tipografia
   151→
   152→| Controle | ID | Tipo | Seletor |
   153→|---|---|---|---|
   154→| Label botão | `label_typography` | TYPOGRAPHY | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-label` |
   155→| Itens menu | `menu_typography` | TYPOGRAPHY | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-menu a` |
   156→
   157→#### Seção: Menu Dropdown
   158→
   159→| Controle | ID | Tipo | Default | Seletor |
   160→|---|---|---|---|---|
   161→| Largura mínima | `menu_min_width` | SLIDER (px/%) | — | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-menu { min-width: {{SIZE}}{{UNIT}}; }` |
   162→| Padding item | `menu_item_padding` | DIMENSIONS | `12px` top/bottom, `20px` left/right | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-menu a { padding: {{TOP}}{{UNIT}} {{RIGHT}}{{UNIT}} {{BOTTOM}}{{UNIT}} {{LEFT}}{{UNIT}}; }` |
   163→| Fundo | `menu_color_bg` | COLOR | — | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-menu { background-color: {{VALUE}}; }` |
   164→| Texto | `menu_color_txt` | COLOR | — | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-menu a { color: {{VALUE}}; }` |
   165→| Texto hover | `menu_color_txt_hv` | COLOR | — | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-menu a:hover { color: {{VALUE}}; }` |
   166→| Fundo hover | `menu_color_bg_hv` | COLOR | — | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-menu a:hover { background-color: {{VALUE}}; }` |
   167→| Cor borda | `menu_color_bdr` | COLOR | — | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-menu { border-color: {{VALUE}}; }` |
   168→| Cor separador | `menu_color_divider` | COLOR | — | `{{WRAPPER}} .dropdown-btn-menu a { border-bottom-color: {{VALUE}}; }` |
   169→
   170→---
   171→
   172→## Render HTML
   173→
   174→```html
   175→<div class="dropdown-btn-wrapper">
   176→  <div class="dropdown-btn-container">
   177→    <button class="dropdown-btn-toggle" type="button">
   178→      <div class="dropdown-btn-content">
   179→        <span class="dropdown-btn-label">DOWNLOAD</span>
   180→        <span class="dropdown-btn-icon">
   181→          <!-- Icons_Manager::render_icon() ou get_default_icon_svg() -->
   182→        </span>
   183→      </div>
   184→    </button>
   185→    <div class="dropdown-btn-menu">
   186→      <!-- link criptografado -->
   187→      <a href="/?jet_download=abc123sha1" rel="nofollow noopener">Português</a>
   188→      <!-- fallback direto (JetElements inativo) -->
   189→      <a href="https://exemplo.com/file.pdf" rel="nofollow noopener">English</a>
   190→    </div>
   191→  </div>
   192→</div>
   193→```
   194→
   195→**Atributos `rel`:**
   196→- Links criptografados (`?jet_download=`): `rel="nofollow noopener"` — o objetivo explícito é impedir rastreamento por buscadores
   197→- Links fallback (URL direta): `rel="nofollow noopener"` igualmente
   198→- Se `item_fallback_url` tiver `is_external: true`: adicionar também `target="_blank"`
   199→
   200→---
   201→
   202→## CSS — Ajustes em `bit-dropdown-btn.css`
   203→
   204→Remover regras hardcoded que serão substituídas pelos seletores Elementor (o controle com `default` garante que o valor sempre existe):
   205→
   206→- `width: 220px` em `.dropdown-btn-container button.dropdown-btn-toggle`
   207→- `padding: 15px 25px` no mesmo seletor
   208→- `border-radius: 4px` nas regras normal e hover do toggle (manter apenas o `border-radius: 4px 4px 0 0` do hover que é lógico de layout)
   209→
   210→Adicionar:
   211→- `.dropdown-btn-icon svg, .dropdown-btn-icon i { display: block; width: 100%; height: 100%; }` — normaliza tanto SVG inline quanto `<i>` de Font Awesome
   212→
   213→---
   214→
   215→## Verificação
   216→
   217→1. No Elementor, buscar "BIT Download Button" → widget aparece na barra lateral
   218→2. Inserir widget → Tab Content: configurar label + 2 links com attachments da biblioteca de mídia
   219→3. Verificar URLs geradas no HTML: devem conter `?jet_download=` (não IDs numéricos diretos)
   220→4. Clicar em link de download → arquivo serve corretamente (sem 404)
   221→5. Hover: dropdown abre, cores mudam, ícone muda de cor
   222→6. Tab Style → todos os controles refletem no preview do Elementor
   223→7. Controle Typography → tipografia aplica nos seletores corretos
   224→8. Fallback: deixar `item_file` vazio, preencher só `item_fallback_url` → link direto funciona com `rel="nofollow noopener"`
   225→9. JetElements inativo: desativar plugin JetElements → widget renderiza usando `item_fallback_url` sem erro fatal (verificar que não há `Call to undefined function jet_elements_download_handler()`)
   226→10. Mirror: confirmar que `docker-dev/common/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.php` e `.css` foram copiados e commitados no repo server-tools
   227→

{
  "file_path": "/Users/dcambria/scripts/server-tools/v2/docker-dev/sites/concertacao/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-03-12-bit-dropdown-btn-v3.md",
  "content": "# BIT Dropdown Button v3.0.0 — Implementation Plan\n\n> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (if subagents available) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.\n\n**Goal:** Atualizar o widget Elementor `bit_dropdown_btn` para usar criptografia de links via JetElements Download Handler, Icon Picker nativo e controles de estilo completos.\n\n**Architecture:** O mu-plugin `bit-dropdown-btn.php` registra um widget Elementor nativo que usa `jet_elements_download_handler()->get_download_link($id)` para gerar URLs ofuscadas `?jet_download=HASH`. O CSS é ajustado para remover valores hardcoded substituídos pelos seletores Elementor.\n\n**Tech Stack:** PHP 8.3, WordPress mu-plugin, Elementor Pro, JetElements (CrocoBl), CSS custom properties.\n\n---\n\n## File Structure\n\n| Arquivo | Responsabilidade |\n|---|---|\n| `wordpress/wp-content/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.php` | Widget Elementor completo + enqueue CSS |\n| `wordpress/wp-content/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.css` | Estilos do componente (sem valores hardcoded de dimensões) |\n| `docker-dev/common/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.php` | Espelho canônico do server-tools |\n| `docker-dev/common/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.css` | Espelho canônico do server-tools |\n\n---\n\n## Chunk 1: CSS — remover hardcoded, normalizar ícone\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `wordpress/wp-content/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.css`\n\n### Task 1: Ajustar CSS\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Abrir o arquivo CSS**\n\n  Arquivo: `wordpress/wp-content/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.css`\n\n  Localizar o bloco `.dropdown-btn-container button.dropdown-btn-toggle` (por volta da linha 28). Ele contém atualmente:\n  ```css\n  width           : 220px;\n  padding         : 15px 25px;\n  border-radius   : 4px;\n  ```\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Remover `width`, `padding` e `border-radius` do toggle (normal)**\n\n  Remover as três linhas abaixo do seletor `.dropdown-btn-container button.dropdown-btn-toggle`:\n  - `width           : 220px;`\n  - `padding         : 15px 25px;`\n  - `border-radius   : 4px;`\n\n  Esses valores passam a ser controlados pelos seletores Elementor com defaults definidos no widget.\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Ajustar `border-radius` no estado hover**\n\n  Localizar `.dropdown-btn-container:hover button.dropdown-btn-toggle`. Ele contém `border-radius: 4px 4px 0 0;` — **manter este**, pois é lógica de layout (achatar a borda inferior quando o menu está aberto), não um valor de design.\n\n  Remover apenas `border-radius: 4px;` do bloco `:focus` e `:focus-visible` se existir como valor duplicado com o normal. Verificar os blocos:\n  - `.dropdown-btn-container button.dropdown-btn-toggle:focus` — remover `border-radius: 4px;`\n  - `.dropdown-btn-container:hover button.dropdown-btn-toggle:focus` — manter `border-radius: 4px 4px 0 0;`\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Atualizar bloco `.dropdown-btn-icon` para suportar SVG inline e Font Awesome**\n\n  Localizar o bloco `.dropdown-btn-container .dropdown-btn-icon` (atual v2.0.0):\n  ```css\n  .dropdown-btn-container .dropdown-btn-icon {\n      display     : inline-flex;\n      align-items : center;\n      width       : 30px;\n      height      : 30px;\n      flex-shrink : 0;\n      color       : var(--btn-normal-icn);\n      transition  : color 0.3s ease;\n  }\n  ```\n\n  Adicionar logo após este bloco a regra de normalização para `svg` e `i` dentro do ícone:\n  ```css\n  .dropdown-btn-container .dropdown-btn-icon svg,\n  .dropdown-btn-container .dropdown-btn-icon i {\n      display : block;\n      width   : 100%;\n      height  : 100%;\n  }\n  ```\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Atualizar cabeçalho do arquivo — versão e descrição**\n\n  Alterar linha 2: `Versão: 2.0.0` → `Versão: 3.0.0`\n\n  Alterar linha 4-10 (bloco de requisitos):\n  ```css\n  /**\n   * BIT Dropdown Button — Estilos\n   * Versão: 3.0.0\n   *\n   * CSS variables do child theme (style.css :root):\n   *   --btn-normal-bg, --btn-normal-txt, --btn-normal-bdr\n   *   --btn-normal-bg-hv, --btn-normal-border-hv, --btn-normal-txt-hv\n   *   --btn-normal-icn, --btn-normal-icon-hv\n   *   --e-global-typography-text-font-family\n   *\n   * Dimensões (width, padding, border-radius) controladas via Elementor.\n   * Ícone: SVG inline ou Font Awesome via Elementor Icon Picker.\n   */\n  ```\n\n- [ ] **Step 6: Verificar sintaxe CSS**\n\n  ```bash\n  # '' );\n                      $attachment_id = (int) ( $link['item_file']['id'] ?? 0 );\n                      $fallback_url  = $link['item_fallback_url']['url'] ?? '';\n                      $is_external   = ! empty( $link['item_fallback_url']['is_external'] );\n\n                      if ( $attachment_id > 0\n                           && function_exists( 'jet_elements_download_handler' )\n                           && jet_elements_download_handler() instanceof Jet_Elements_Download_Handler ) {\n                          // Link criptografado via JetElements\n                          $href   = esc_url( jet_elements_download_handler()->get_download_link( $attachment_id ) );\n                          $target = '';\n                      } elseif ( ! empty( $fallback_url ) ) {\n                          // URL direta (fallback)\n                          $href   = esc_url( $fallback_url );\n                          $target = $is_external ? ' target=\"_blank\"' : '';\n                      } else {\n                          continue; // nenhuma URL válida — não renderiza este item\n                      }\n\n                      echo '<a href=\"' . $href . '\" rel=\"nofollow noopener\"' . $target . '>'\n                         . $item_label . '</a>';\n                  }\n                  echo '</div>'; // .dropdown-btn-menu\n              }\n\n              echo '</div>'; // .dropdown-btn-container\n              echo '</div>'; // Na aba Conteúdo, configurar 2 itens no repeater, selecionando arquivos PDF da biblioteca de mídia no campo \"Arquivo (biblioteca de mídia)\".\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Inspecionar HTML gerado**\n\n  No preview, clicar com botão direito → \"Inspecionar\". Verificar que os links `<a>` contêm `?jet_download=` e **não** IDs numéricos diretos.\n\n  ```\n  # Esperado:\n  <a href=\"http://site.local/?jet_download=abc123sha1hash\" rel=\"nofollow noopener\">Português</a>\n\n  # Não esperado:\n  <a href=\"/wp-content/uploads/arquivo.pdf\">Português</a>\n  ```\n\n- [ ] **Step 6: Testar hover e dropdown**\n\n  Passar o mouse sobre o botão no preview. Esperado: dropdown aparece, cores mudam, ícone muda de cor.\n\n- [ ] **Step 7: Testar controles de estilo**\n\n  Na aba Estilo, alterar cada seção:\n  - Botão — Dimensões: alterar largura → botão redimensiona no preview\n  - Cores — Normal: alterar fundo → cor muda\n  - Ícone: trocar para outro ícone via picker → ícone muda\n  - Tipografia: alterar fonte do label → fonte muda\n  - Menu Dropdown: alterar padding dos itens → espaçamento muda\n\n- [ ] **Step 8: Testar fallback URL**\n\n  Editar um item do repeater: limpar o campo \"Arquivo\", preencher apenas \"URL alternativa\". Verificar no HTML que o link aparece como URL direta (`href=\"https://...\"`), não criptografada.\n\n- [ ] **Step 9: Testar fallback JetElements inativo (opcional)**\n\n  Desativar o plugin JetElements via WP Admin → Plugins. Recarregar o preview do Elementor. Esperado: widget renderiza sem erro PHP fatal (verificar logs em `logs/php/error.log`). Os links com attachment ID devem desaparecer (nenhuma URL → item pulado), e os links com `item_fallback_url` devem aparecer normalmente.\n\n  Reativar JetElements ao finalizar.\n\n- [ ] **Step 10: Confirmar ícone padrão**\n\n  Quando o controle de ícone estiver com o default `eicon-download` (Elementor icons), verificar se o ícone renderiza. Se a library `eicons` não retornar HTML (possível em alguns ambientes locais sem Font Awesome carregado), o SVG fallback deve aparecer. Verificar no HTML:\n  - Com eicons carregado: `<i class=\"eicon-download\" aria-hidden=\"true\"></i>` dentro de `.dropdown-btn-icon`\n  - Com fallback SVG: `<svg ... fill-rule=\"evenodd\" ...>` dentro de `.dropdown-btn-icon`\n"
}

- Mirror to `docker-dev/common/mu-plugins/` is covered in Chunk 3, Task 3 with separate commit in the server-tools repo.

     1→# BIT Dropdown Button v3.0.0 — Implementation Plan
     2→
     3→> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (if subagents available) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
     4→
     5→**Goal:** Atualizar o widget Elementor `bit_dropdown_btn` para usar criptografia de links via JetElements Download Handler, Icon Picker nativo e controles de estilo completos.
     6→
     7→**Architecture:** O mu-plugin `bit-dropdown-btn.php` registra um widget Elementor nativo que usa `jet_elements_download_handler()->get_download_link($id)` para gerar URLs ofuscadas `?jet_download=HASH`. O CSS é ajustado para remover valores hardcoded substituídos pelos seletores Elementor.
     8→
     9→**Tech Stack:** PHP 8.3, WordPress mu-plugin, Elementor Pro, JetElements (CrocoBl), CSS custom properties.
    10→
    11→---
    12→
    13→## File Structure
    14→
    15→| Arquivo | Responsabilidade |
    16→|---|---|
    17→| `wordpress/wp-content/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.php` | Widget Elementor completo + enqueue CSS |
    18→| `wordpress/wp-content/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.css` | Estilos do componente (sem valores hardcoded de dimensões) |
    19→| `docker-dev/common/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.php` | Espelho canônico do server-tools |
    20→| `docker-dev/common/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.css` | Espelho canônico do server-tools |
    21→
    22→---
    23→
    24→## Chunk 1: CSS — remover hardcoded, normalizar ícone
    25→
    26→**Files:**
    27→- Modify: `wordpress/wp-content/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.css`
    28→
    29→### Task 1: Ajustar CSS
    30→
    31→- [ ] **Step 1: Abrir o arquivo CSS**
    32→
    33→  Arquivo: `wordpress/wp-content/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.css`
    34→
    35→  Localizar o bloco `.dropdown-btn-container button.dropdown-btn-toggle` (por volta da linha 28). Ele contém atualmente:
    36→  ```css
    37→  width           : 220px;
    38→  padding         : 15px 25px;
    39→  border-radius   : 4px;
    40→  ```
    41→
    42→- [ ] **Step 2: Remover `width`, `padding` e `border-radius` do toggle (normal)**
    43→
    44→  Remover as três linhas abaixo do seletor `.dropdown-btn-container button.dropdown-btn-toggle`:
    45→  - `width           : 220px;`
    46→  - `padding         : 15px 25px;`
    47→  - `border-radius   : 4px;`
    48→
    49→  Esses valores passam a ser controlados pelos seletores Elementor com defaults definidos no widget.
    50→
    51→- [ ] **Step 3: Ajustar `border-radius` no estado hover**
    52→
    53→  Localizar `.dropdown-btn-container:hover button.dropdown-btn-toggle`. Ele contém `border-radius: 4px 4px 0 0;` — **manter este**, pois é lógica de layout (achatar a borda inferior quando o menu está aberto), não um valor de design.
    54→

   494→          protected function render() {
   495→              $s     = $this->get_settings_for_display();
   496→              $label = esc_html( ! empty( $s['label'] ) ? $s['label'] : 'Download' );
   497→              $links = ! empty( $s['links'] ) ? $s['links'] : [];
   498→
   499→              echo '<div class="dropdown-btn-wrapper">';
   500→              echo '<div class="dropdown-btn-container">';
   501→              echo '<button class="dropdown-btn-toggle" type="button">';
   502→              echo '<div class="dropdown-btn-content">';
   503→              echo '<span class="dropdown-btn-label">' . $label . '</span>';
   504→
   505→              // Ícone — tenta Icon Picker, cai para SVG padrão
   506→              echo '<span class="dropdown-btn-icon">';
   507→              $icon_settings = $s['icon'] ?? [];
   508→              $icon_html     = '';
   509→              if ( ! empty( $icon_settings['value'] ) ) {
   510→                  ob_start();
   511→                  \Elementor\Icons_Manager::render_icon( $icon_settings, [ 'aria-hidden' => 'true' ] );
   512→                  $icon_html = ob_get_clean();
   513→              }
   514→              echo ! empty( $icon_html ) ? $icon_html : $this->get_default_icon_svg(); // phpcs:ignore WordPress.Security.EscapeOutput.OutputNotEscaped
   515→              echo '</span>';
   516→
   517→              echo '</div>'; // .dropdown-btn-content
   518→              echo '</button>';
   519→
   520→              // Menu dropdown
   521→              if ( ! empty( $links ) ) {
   522→                  echo '<div class="dropdown-btn-menu">';
   523→                  foreach ( $links as $link ) {
   524→                      $item_label    = esc_html( $link['item_label'] ?? '' );
   525→                      $attachment_id = (int) ( $link['item_file']['id'] ?? 0 );
   526→                      $fallback_url  = $link['item_fallback_url']['url'] ?? '';
   527→                      $is_external   = ! empty( $link['item_fallback_url']['is_external'] );
   528→
   529→                      if ( $attachment_id > 0
   530→                           && function_exists( 'jet_elements_download_handler' )
   531→                           && jet_elements_download_handler() instanceof Jet_Elements_Download_Handler ) {
   532→                          // Link criptografado via JetElements
   533→                          $href   = esc_url( jet_elements_download_handler()->get_download_link( $attachment_id ) );
   534→                          $target = '';
   535→                      } elseif ( ! empty( $fallback_url ) ) {
   536→                          // URL direta (fallback)
   537→                          $href   = esc_url( $fallback_url );
   538→                          $target = $is_external ? ' target="_blank"' : '';
   539→                      } else {
   540→                          continue; // nenhuma URL válida — não renderiza este item
   541→                      }
   542→
   543→                      echo '<a href="' . $href . '" rel="nofollow noopener"' . $target . '>'
   544→                         . $item_label . '</a>';
   545→                  }
   546→                  echo '</div>'; // .dropdown-btn-menu
   547→              }
   548→
   549→              echo '</div>'; // .dropdown-btn-container
   550→              echo '</div>'; // .dropdown-btn-wrapper
   551→          }
   552→      }
   553→
   554→      $widgets_manager->register( new Bit_Dropdown_Btn_Widget() );
   555→  } );
   556→  ```

---

## Code Review: Task 1 — CSS adjustments to bit-dropdown-btn.css

### What was done well

All five planned steps were correctly executed in a single commit (`0e10a8d8d`). The CSS syntax is clean, alignment formatting is consistent with the existing project style, and the specificity comment on line 29 was preserved. The `border-radius: 4px 4px 0 0` on the hover state was correctly kept — this is layout logic, not a design value, and the plan explicitly required it to remain. The standalone `border-radius: 4px` on the `:focus` / `:focus-visible` block was correctly removed. The Step 6 verification check (zero results for `border-radius: 4px;` in isolation) passes.

---

### Critical Issues

None.

---

### Important Issues

**1. Canonical mirror (`common/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.css`) is out of sync.**

The plan's Chunk 3 / Task 3 explicitly requires mirroring both files to `docker-dev/common/mu-plugins/`. The CSS mirror at `/Users/dcambria/scripts/server-tools/v2/docker-dev/common/mu-plugins/bit-dropdown-btn.css` still contains the v2.0.0 content — it still has `width: 220px`, `padding: 15px 25px`, `border-radius: 4px` on the toggle normal state, `border-radius: 4px` on the focus block, and it lacks the `svg, i` normalization rule. This is the state from before Task 1 ran.

The plan commits were intended to be done per-chunk. Since Task 1 only committed the site copy, the mirror copy was left behind. This is not a problem for runtime (WordPress loads from the site path, not the common mirror), but it violates the CLAUDE.md rule: "Todo mu-plugin criado ou modificado DEVE ser commitado em `common/mu-plugins/`". The mirror will mislead any future agent that reads `common/mu-plugins/` as the source of truth.

This needs to be fixed when Chunk 3 / Task 3 runs. If Tasks 2 and 3 have not yet been executed, the mirror copy must be synced as part of that step.

---

### Suggestions

**2. The icon normalization rule (`svg, i`) interacts with the `icon_size` slider in the PHP widget.**

The normalization rule sets `width: 100%; height: 100%` on `svg` and `i`. The PHP widget's `icon_size` control (planned in Task 2 / Chunk 2) targets the same elements with `width: {{SIZE}}{{UNIT}}; height: {{SIZE}}{{UNIT}}`. Since Elementor inline styles have higher specificity than the mu-plugin stylesheet, the slider will override `100%` correctly. However, the icon container (`.dropdown-btn-icon`) has fixed `width: 30px; height: 30px` in the CSS (lines 81–82). The `icon_size` slider targets the inner `svg/i`, not the container. If a user sets `icon_size` to, say, 48px, the inner element will try to render at 48px but the container is capped at 30px — `overflow: hidden` is not set on `.dropdown-btn-icon`, so the icon will overflow the container boundaries and potentially misalign the flex layout. This is acceptable for the current scope since Task 2 hasn't landed yet, but the PHP widget should document that `icon_size` controls the inner element and that `.dropdown-btn-icon`'s fixed dimensions in the CSS serve as the visual bounding box.

**3. The `font-size: 16px` and `font-weight: 600` values on the toggle button (lines 38–39) remain hardcoded.**

The plan only required removing `width`, `padding`, and `border-radius`. Keeping these typography values in CSS is consistent with the plan scope. However, the PHP widget (planned for Task 2) includes a `label_typography` group control that will generate Elementor inline selectors targeting `.dropdown-btn-label`, not the `button` element directly. The `font-size: 16px` on the button will cascade down to `.dropdown-btn-label` unless the Elementor typography control overrides it. This should work correctly, but it is worth noting that `font-size` and `font-weight` are effectively the last hardcoded values inside the toggle rule — they could be candidates for future removal once the typography control is verified in Task 2.
